### 4. Added value of a second phase and longer horizons

LECZ-CARE served and pre-IJL 2014-2018 period as a powerful incubation period during which the various researchers initiated partnerships and set up new research themes with HCMUT partners (hydrology, eutrophication, remediation and coastal erosion). During this period, several technicians and the first PhD students were trained, the CARE laboratory started to acquire basic field monitoring equipment. Monitoring of the river was initiated for several parameters and the water level for hydrodynamics., LECZ-CARE has been quite productive (more than 70 papers have been produced) and allowed to test and to demonstrate a successful partnership implement with HCMUT. The promotion of the LECZ-CARE project results advances their careers and enhance their capacity to attract further research funding. With the renewal of IJL-2 we aim to strengthen the research on innovative research topics related to emerging threats for water quality (such as emerging contaminants, microplastics, antibiotic resistance genes, urbanization, climate change) and also resilience strategies (nature based solutions). As mentioned previously, IJL-2 aims to extend the research scope by adding new research topics and in particular to include social sciences and human sciences perspectives. We aim to generate connection between the different research actions, to create more interdisciplinary sciences. We will mutualize field campaign, establish long term monitoring along the Saigon Dong Nai and also investigate remediation strategies for a broad range of pollutants at the same time (microplastics, emerging contaminants, nutrient) to provide a unique database integrating the “one health perspective” for water quality. This approach too rare will be a real asset for the understanding of the impact of urbanization on water degradation and its adverse effect on ecosystem health. While research on Water is the connecting thread among the different axis, we will also initiate new research to investigate the distribution of pollutants within different environmental compartments (water, sediment, air, groundwater and biota). While LECZ-CARE allowed to train a first generation of bright PhD students (Tran Hai Yen, Nguyen An, Truong Quoc Thanh, Nguyen Ngoc Thi Tuyet), it is important to mention that all these young researchers will be involved in CARE (Tran Hai Yen is employed at HCMUT and leader of CT$_4$-WP11, Thanh Truong Quoc is a lecturer at HCMUT and will be involved in CT$_1$- WP$_2$, Nguyen Ngoc Thi Tuyet is a research engineer who will be working for SAWACO project and she is involved in CT$_2$-WP$_5$, and Nguyen An is a postdoc in TROPECOS project and he is involved in CTP$_2$-WP$_4$), they will be the driving force of the IJL and they will contribute to seal the partnership. The expatriation plan over the IJL phase 2 period (5 researchers) and the funded research projects will significantly contribute to the success of CARE. [SOURCE: Page_69]

3. Ambition of the project, particularly in terms of regional and international visibility; link with the structuring actions of the other institutions involved. [SOURCE: Page_69]

While IJL-1 was limited to the CARE and HCMUT perimeter to structure the partnership, IJL-2 aims to extend its network locally to other institutes in HCMC, such as HCMUSSH (Axis 5), HCMUS (Axis 2) and HCMUNRE (Axis 1), in line with the broadening of research topics and research competencies. In view of our work on a regional scale (coastal erosion, sea level rise, water pollution, population practices) and our funded research projects in the Mekong delta, we will initiate collaboration with the University of Can Tho, Ben Tre and the ITC in Cambodia to increase our visibility and partnership at a regional scale. As mentioned previously, all students from the region will be invited to attend our summer school. Through the IJL, we will animate and support local research networks between HCMC and other universities in Can Tho and Ben Tre regions (Mekong Delta region) and also ITC in Cambodia. Our research and training activities dedicated to HCMC city region and Mekong Delta as well as the expansion of the IJL perimeter will serve to consolidate the role of CARE laboratory as a future sub-regional center of excellence at the regional scale. A scientific hub where international and Vietnamese researchers can meet and work together. French institutions (CNRS, and G-INP) are eager to support CARE but only if CARE is labeled IJL to structure the research through this IRD partnership framework. In addition, IJL CARE will ask a new French partner, INRAE, to support the IJL for bringing new perspective on NBS and water related issues (“AQUA” department at INRAE). CARE would then involve researchers from all these French research institutions and will serve as a scientific base which will be beneficial for all partners. CARE will also involve few international researchers and aims to bright through such connection. [SOURCE: Page_69]

During the next 5 years we will anticipate the ending of IJL program (if renewed) and secure the economic model of CARE structure for a bright future. We believe that this goal can be achieved if several conditions are met in order for CARE to become a well-known centre. In particular, the continuity of support from the Vietnamese academic authorities should be obtained through an annual budget from the VNU and through several permanent scientific and administrative staff officially linked to the CARE building. [SOURCE: Page_69]

In addition, the multilateral exchange and the provision of modern scientific equipment will be enhanced with the continuous support of the Rescif network. Indeed, we strongly believe that the CARE needs a significant effort from IJL institutions and Vietnamese laboratories to increase the laboratory facilities to purchase up-to-date equipments. We cannot be internationally recognized without clear and easy access to state of the art equipment [SOURCE: Page_69]

Finally, the future will also depend on the opportunities for scientists to apply for international calls that can include Vietnam as a partner. [SOURCE: Page_69]

68 [SOURCE: Page_69]

Project appendices Submitted Research Projects [SOURCE: Page_70]

ANR Ladybird Project (2024-2028) 777 k EUR (under reviewed): The Ladybird project proposes to set up an environmental monitoring system based on participatory science to predict the risk of flooding in Ho Chi Minh City. Flooding results from three simultaneous phenomena: heavy rainfall, the flow of the Dong Nai and Saigon rivers, and the influence of high tides on the latter. The submergence of HCMC, which is sinking by more than 3 centimeters a year in some areas (subsidence), considerably increases the risks. The Ladybird project plans to use low-cost IoT (Internet of Things) components to create a flood monitoring system involving commercial vessels operating on the Saigon and Dong Nai rivers to measure water levels continuously. These measurements, combined with rainfall measurements, will be used to map water levels on the rivers in real-time, providing an early warning system for the project's Vietnamese partners. This project will involve French academic and industrial partners, in collaboration with the universities of HCMC (HMCUT, HMCUNRE) and the South Vietnam Meteorological Department, SRHMC, and the Hydrological Studies Department, SIHYMECC. The REX AGUA project (2023-2024) 5 K (under reviewed) aims to compare the environmental monitoring policies of three cities - Lyon (France), Ho Chi Minh City (Vietnam) and La Paz-El Alto (Bolivia) - with the aim of proposing improvements to make them more efficient. In all three cases, it is more precisely a question of focusing on water management, which constitutes a cross-cutting theme in the environment. This angle of approach makes it possible to show the progress made by the policies for monitoring this resource in three very different contexts from the outset, while underlining the path that still remains to be covered to guarantee the sustainability of this vital resource, advocated in particular by different Sustainable Development Goals (SDG$_6$ Clean Water and Sanitation, SDG13, SDG15, SDG11, SDG$_2$, for the main ones) and their achievement indicators (https://unstats.un.org/sdgs/indicators/indicators-list). [SOURCE: Page_70]

Dr. Nguyen Phuoc Dan, Professor [SOURCE: Page_75]

74 [SOURCE: Page_75]

BADUEL CHRISTINE, Environmental and Analytical Chemist RESEARCHER AT FRENCH RESEARCH INSTITUTE FOR SUSTAINABLE DEVELOPMENT (IRD) Based at IGE, Institute of Geosciences and Environment, Grenoble, France RESEARCHER IDENTIFIER: Web of Science ResearcherID H-4066-2016 Language: French (mother tongue) & English (Professional fluent) [SOURCE: Page_76]

FIELD OF SPECIALIZATION / SKILLS C. Baduel is a researcher in environmental and analytical chemistry, she studies the behavior, fate and bioavailability of emerging chemicals (PFASs, Pharmaceuticals, Benzotriazoles, etc.) in the environment. To achieve these goals, her research includes the development and the application of analytical methods to quantify emerging contaminants at trace level in environmental and biological matrices using state of the art mass spectrometry techniques (LC-MS/MS, GC- MS/MS). She also developed and applied analytical strategies for the identification of new contaminants and unknown (bio)degradation products using high resolution mass spectrometry LC-HRMS/MS (suspect screening, non-target analysis, identification of unknowns). Since she joined IRD in January 2018, her research has been focusing on assessing the transfer, behavior and bioavailability of emerging organic contaminants in tropical aquatic system. She has developed strong collaboration in Vietnam, Cambodia and Philippines. [SOURCE: Page_76]

EMPLOYMENT  2018: Researcher at IRD, the French Research Institute for Sustainable Development and based at the Institute of Environmental Geosciences (IGE), Grenoble, FRANCE. Since 2018, 5 research visits (a total of 8 months including 3 MLDs) at CARE and HCMUT in Vietnam despite 2 years of impossible trip due to COVID outbreak  2016 -2017: Postdoc self-funded at the Institute of Analytical Sciences (ISA), CNRS, Université de Lyon 1, ENS de Lyon, France. Including a 6 months’research visit at Toxicological Center à Antwerpen (Belgique). Team Leader: Pr. A. Covaci  2012- 2016: Postdoc at National Research Centre for Environmental Toxicology (ENTOX), Renamed QAEHS, The University of Queensland, Australia. Team Leader: Pr. J. Mueller  2010 - 2011: Postdoctoral at the Institute of Analytical Chemistry and Environmental Sciences ACES, University of Stockholm, Sweden. Team Leader: Dr Nozière B.  2006 – 2010 : PhD au Laboratoire de Glaciologie et Géophysique de l’Environnement (LGGE), Université Joseph Fournier, Grenoble, France. LGGE/CNRS, Pr Voisin Pr Legrand. [SOURCE: Page_76]

TEACHING AND TRAINING ACTIVITIES  2022-2023: Organization of a 1-week training course for Cambodian researchers in pesticides analysis in environmental samples (Projet WAT’Health)  2021, 2022, 2023: A lecture for PhD students at Uppsala university on Fate of emerging pollutants  2012, 2013, 2014 & 2015: Coordinator and leader of the field camp for the Undergraduate students module “Environmental Toxicology and Monitoring" à l’université du Queensland, Brisbane, Australia.  2012-2013: Creation, development, and organization of the new module “Environmental Toxicology and Monitoring (code ENVM3211)”at the university of Queensland  2006 & 2007: Lecturer for practical work in general chemistry and inorganic chemistry at the University Joseph Fourier, Grenoble, France (130h). 4. Estimated budget showing the financial contributions of each stakeholders to the overall implementation of the project as well as specific needs foreseen (equipment, infrastructures, etc.).* [SOURCE: Page_82]

In table A$_2$, we have summarized the different activities that need to be funded in order to fulfill the different objectives and tasks of our IJL. In Table A$_3$, we have indicated the former financial contributions of each stakeholder to LECZ-CARE (2019-2023) and to the future CARE (2024-2029) if granted. We have displayed also the expected contribution of the Vietnam side as “VNU” grants. As usual, both VNU (Vietnam and French sides of the budget are displayed at the same time in order to give a clear visibility of the financial fluxes [SOURCE: Page_82]

Table A$_2$: Financial need per year for CARE IJL (2024-2029 period) [SOURCE: Page_82]

Financial                          More Details Activities need per year Infrastructure and staff          20,000 1 Staff dedicated to look after the Human Resources for                                internet website and to prepare the data research (assistants for             20,000        management plan, Staff to perform sampling field and lab)                                and analysis for long term monitoring, 2 Staff to participate on field trip and for survey International Missions            10,000               Short Missions to France or to Vietnam Support for MLD and                                     Support for field work dedicated to Expatriation                   30,000             researchers in MLD or in Expatriation and PhD surveys Sampling, analytical cost to monitor Long term Monitoring               7,000 nutrients, 25,000              Seed money to support research project YIPP Project / seed (5k EUR each), Projects are submitted and money reviewed by external researchers. 15,000              Maintenance of all equipment belonging Consumable and small                            to CARE laboratory and basic consumable for equipment                                     the laboratory : gloves, lab coats, aluminum foil, solvents, Extra events :                 5,000                  Financial support to yearly CARE’s workshop, conference,                                   scientific days and to bi-annual WSGT scientific days                                 conferences organized by our local partners Summer School for                  5,000             1 week summer school on “Water quality South East Asia Students                                         and Emerging Threats” Total                     125,000 [SOURCE: Page_82]

The main expenses are the infrastructure costs for CARE building (20 k EUR) and the human resources dedicated to research activities (20 k EUR). The infrastructure costs include costs related to the building (furniture, painting, repairs, etc.) and the staff dedicated to the building (admin staff, gardener, cleaning staff etc.) it is mainly due to the VNU-HCMUT grant. Regarding the human resources dedicated to the research (20 k EUR). We need staff to carry out bi-monthly monitoring which includes water sampling and biochemical analysis (nutrients, CECs, microplastics etc..) for our long-term monitoring of the water quality in HCMC. The staff will also be responsible for the air sampling campaign that will take place throughout the year at HCMUS. We will also need temporary staff to participate in specific sampling campaigns throughout the year and to field surveys to support the activities of the social scientists. We will need a part-time staff to work on the data management plan and to support researchers in putting their data online on Datasud. Another part-time staff will be dedicated to the CARE website and to look after the Facebook account. [SOURCE: Page_82]

Another cost is related to the international missions (10k EUR) from Vietnam to France or from France to Vietnam for researchers or guest researchers (e.g. advisory board researchers for example, etc.). It can be also used for national conferences. [SOURCE: Page_82]

We will also have a part of the HCMUT money (10-15k EUR) that will be used as reward for novel research paper according to HCMUT’s new rule. Every year there will be an internal call for Vietnamese researchers. The objective of this internal call is to stimulate research ideas and to promote collaboration and multidisciplinary research within the IJL circle. All faculties can apply. [SOURCE: Page_82]

We would also like to allocate functioning and seed money to researchers, Postdoc, PhD students who are going on MLD (to Vietnam or to France) or who are going on an expatriation to help them to set up the research with their partners. 81 [SOURCE: Page_82]

We also need financial support to carry out the bi-monthly monitoring, including water sampling and very costly bio- chemical analysis (nutrients, 150 CECs, microplastics etc..) to provide long-term monitoring of the water and air quality in HCMC through the duration of the IJL. We will also need financial support to maintain the CARE lab (maintenance of all equipment) and to buy basic consumables to run the lab (gloves, sampling bottles etc…), the remaining money will be used to replace or to buy new small equipment (multi-parameter probe, vortex, ultrasonic bath etc.) [SOURCE: Page_83]

Finally, we will save 5k EUR to support yearly CARE’s events such as the Scientific Days, or to support the WSGT conference organized every 2 years by our closest partners. [SOURCE: Page_83]

After five years of operation of LECZ-CARE, we believe that even if this budget was found enough for the 2020- 2021 period (during the lockdown and zero-COVID period), for 2022 and 2023 this budget remains far too low to support all activities and to initiate new activities such as air quality monitoring; human sciences activities; high impact precipitation; relative sea level rise prediction, nature based solution and the microplastics research activities. [SOURCE: Page_83]

The research scope of CARE IJL has increased significantly, as has the budget. Thus, our financial needs and our request for financial support have increased as shown in the Table A$_3$. [SOURCE: Page_83]
